<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every finite connected planar graph with arbitrary positive real edge lengths embeds into real <i>L</i><sub>1</sub> with a universal distortion bound. This resolves the planar embedding conjecture positively.

- **claim-planar-l1-universal-distortion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a finite universal constant $C\ge1$ with the following property. For every finite connected planar graph $G=(V,E)$ with strictly positive real edge lengths, there exist a measure space $(\Omega,\mathcal F,\mu)$ and a map $f:V\to L_1(\Omega,\mathcal F,\mu;\mathbb R)$ such that $$d_G(x,y)\le\\|f(x)-f(y)\\|_1\le C d_G(x,y) \qquad(x,y\in V).$$
  - Explanation: A single universal constant bounds the L1 distortion of every finite connected planar graph metric with positive real edge lengths, independently of vertex count and edge lengths.
- **claim-column-shortest-paths** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-geodesics)): The function $d_I$ is a metric on each component of $X_I$. Its finite distances are attained by paths using each column at most once. Components of $X_I$ correspond exactly to components of $G_I$. If $I$ is bounded, each component of $X_I$ is compact. For arbitrary $I$, bounded closed subsets of each component are compact. In particular distance from a point to a nonempty compact set is attained.
  - Explanation: Inside a height interval the intrinsic distance is a genuine metric on each component of the column space, finite distances are attained by paths using each column at most once, and components match components of the active column graph.
- **claim-planar-column-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-realization)): Every finite connected planar graph with positive edge lengths has an isometric realization of its geometric edge metric in a noncrossing column model satisfying the terminal-switch condition. The added parts of the model consist of separate infinite tails and do not shorten distances in the original graph.
  - Explanation: A shortest-path tree with doubled non-tree edges gives a noncrossing column model that realizes the planar graph metric isometrically, with the added tails never shortening original distances.
- **claim-chord-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-chord-separation)): Let $uv$ be an edge of a noncrossing circular graph. Any graph path from the strict interior of one circular $u$–$v$ arc to the strict interior of the other visits $u$ or $v$.
  - Explanation: In a noncrossing circular graph an edge separates the strict interiors of its two circular arcs: any path between them must touch an endpoint of the edge.
- **claim-gaps-of-connected-set** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-gap)): Let $S$ be a nonempty connected vertex set in a noncrossing circular graph. Every graph path avoiding $S$ lies in one of the open circular gaps between consecutive vertices of $S$. Here the complement of a singleton is regarded as one gap. Connectivity of $S$ and the path may be witnessed using different subgraphs of the same noncrossing graph.
  - Explanation: A path avoiding a connected vertex set in a noncrossing circular graph stays inside a single open circular gap of that set.
- **claim-skipping-a-column** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-skipping)): Give an open circular arc its linear order, and assume its complementary closed arc contains a column index. Let a vertex walk stay in the open arc, and let an index $i$ lie strictly between its initial and final indices. Either the walk visits $i$, or one of its edges has endpoints strictly on opposite sides of $i$. In the latter case the edge is a non-neighbor link. If a second path starts strictly between the endpoints of this edge and leaves that strict interval, it visits an endpoint of the edge.
  - Explanation: A walk in an open arc either visits an intermediate column or uses a non-neighbor link straddling it, and a path leaving that link's interval must meet an endpoint of the link.
- **claim-active-graph-outerplanar** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-outerplanar)): Every active graph $G_I$ is outerplanar and has no $K_{2,3}$ minor.
  - Explanation: Every active column graph is outerplanar and has no K_{2,3} minor, which is the structural fact behind the two-portal estimate.
- **claim-distance-sublevel-connected** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-sublevel)): Fix a component of $X_I$, a source $o$ in it, and a height $H\in I$. Put $q_i=d_I(o,(i,H))$, taking $q_i=+\infty$ outside that component. For every real $t$, the nonempty set $\{i:q_i\le t\}$ is connected in $G_I$.
  - Explanation: Sublevel sets of the distance profile from a source at a fixed height induce connected subgraphs of the active column graph.
- **claim-two-portals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-portals)): Let $C$ be a component of the subgraph of $G_J$ induced by $\{i:b_i\ge t\}$. An *entrance column* of $C$ is an index in $C$ adjacent to an index with radial value below $t$. If entrance columns exist, there are at most two of their midpoints, called portals, such that every point on every entrance column lies within $200a$ in $d_J$ of one of the portals. Every portal has radial value in $[t,t+a)$. If $t>a/2$ and $C$ is nonempty, entrance columns exist.
  - Explanation: The entrances to a connected high radial component are covered by at most two portal points up to a bounded distance, because three separated entrances would force a K_{2,3} minor.
- **claim-radial-cell-diameter** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-cells)): Assume $a\le D/1000$. Every radial cell is compact and path-connected, and has weak diameter at most $10D$ in $d_J$. Its frontier is compact, possibly empty.
  - Explanation: For a thin enough band every radial cell is compact, path-connected and has weak diameter bounded by a fixed multiple of the cell width, with a compact frontier.
- **claim-radial-padding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-padding)): Let $v=(i,H)\in X_J$, and let $T=\theta+D\mathbb Z$ be the radial threshold set. If $$\label{geo:padding-condition} \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(b_i,T)>\rho+2a,$$ then every point reachable from $v$ by a band path of length at most $\rho$ has all its representatives in the same cell as $i$. Moreover $d_J(v,\partial C)>\rho$ for that cell.* *For uniform $\theta\in[0,D)$, the probability that <a href="#geo:padding-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:padding-condition">[geo:padding-condition]</a> fails is at most $$\min\{1,\,2(\rho+2a)/D\}.$$ There is no factor for the number of representatives of $v$: their radial values differ pairwise by at most $a$.* *In particular, if $a\le D/1000$ and the radial values of two points are at distance greater than $0.02D$ from the thresholds, then their distances to their respective frontiers are greater than $0.01D$. If they belong to different cells, their mutual $d_J$-distance is greater than $0.01D$.
  - Explanation: A point whose radial value is far from the thresholds keeps all short band paths inside its cell and stays far from the frontier; the failure probability over a random offset is linear in the path length.
- **claim-successive-portal-levels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-portal-levels)): Fix a step $\delta>1000a$. At depth $n\ge1$ take the components of $\{b_i\ge n\delta\}$ and give each its entrance portals from Lemma <a href="#geo:portals" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:portals">10</a>. The depth-zero node is the whole component, with its sole portal $o$. Each positive-depth node has a unique parent. For a node at depth $n$, every point on one of its columns with $b_i<(n+1)\delta$, and every portal of one of its children, is within $\delta+220a$ of one of its own portals. Each child portal is at distance at least $\delta-10a$ from every own portal.
  - Explanation: The superlevel components at successive thresholds form a rooted tree whose portals are at controlled distances from the parent's portals, both above and below.
- **claim-vertical-control-rebasing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sets-vertical)): For two points whose sets are expressed in common chart variables, $$\label{sets:distance} \mu(\mathcal A_v\mathbin\triangle\mathcal A_w) =\max\{\|h(v)-h(w)\|,\kappa\|P(v)-P(w)\|\}.$$ If $\kappa\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}_{\mathrm{vert}}(P)<1$ and $P$ respects switches, the sets are nested increasingly on each column interval in the chart. Their symmetric-difference measure there equals the vertical length.* *A branch $P(v)=\psi(h(v))$, where $\psi:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ is Lipschitz with $\kappa\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(\psi)<1$, can be represented by a fresh constant chart without changing any sets.
  - Explanation: Label-set symmetric difference is the maximum of the height and scaled field differences; a small vertical field slope gives nested sets with measure equal to vertical length, and height-only fields can be rebased to a constant chart.
- **claim-component-refinement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sets-component-refinement)): Suppose a system of sets is nested increasingly on each column, respects switches, and has symmetric-difference measure at most vertical length. It defines an $L_1$ pseudometric dominated by $d$. It also admits the following refinement with the same upper bound.* *For a label $\omega$, join two columns when one of their switches occurs at a height active for $\omega$. To an active point assign the unit vector indexed by its component in this finite graph, and to an inactive point assign zero. Integrating the $\ell_1$ distances of these vectors gives the refined pseudometric. It dominates the original symmetric-difference pseudometric.* *If a non-neighbor link has inactive tip and two active points have representatives strictly on opposite sides of it, their refined distance for that label is $2$.
  - Explanation: Replacing membership by active-component coordinates yields a refined L1 pseudometric with the same upper bound that dominates the original one and gives a fixed value when an inactive tip separates two active points.
- **claim-thin-band-interpolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-int-interpolation)): Let $0<\zeta<1$, let $E>0$, and set $$\label{int:constants} \begin{gathered} \delta=E/8,\qquad a_0=10^{-6}\zeta\delta,\qquad q=\zeta\delta/20,\\ \rho_0=\zeta\delta/1000,\qquad M=\lceil15/\zeta\rceil+3,\qquad N=6(M+1). \end{gathered}$$ Suppose $0<a\le a_0$, and let $p:Y\to\mathbb R$ be $(1-\zeta)$-Lipschitz for $d_J$. There is a random function $Q:Y\to\mathbb R$, obtained using finitely many independent scalar noises uniform on $[q,2q]$, with the following properties.* 1. *For every realization, $Q$ respects all switches, is $1$-Lipschitz for $d_J$, and satisfies $\|Q(v)-p(v)\|\le6\delta<E$.* 2. *Its pieces use finitely many named atoms of the form $$\label{int:atom-form} A(v)=C+\varepsilon d_J(k,v),\qquad \varepsilon\in\{-1,1\},\quad k\in Y.$$ Each name specifies a source $k$ and sign $\varepsilon$ fixed before the noises are sampled; the intercept $C$ is random. Whenever the construction reuses an atom, it retains its name.* 3. *For a fixed $x\in Y$ and $0<\rho\le\rho_0$, there is a list $\mathcal A(x,\rho)$ of at most $N$ atom names and sources, determined without the noises, that contains the name of every atom selected by $Q$ on $B_{d_J}(x,\rho)$. Except on an event of probability at most $$\label{int:probability} \frac{N^2}{q}(8\rho+\eta),$$ one named atom is selected throughout this ball.* *Here $\eta=0$ for continuous uniform noises. Alternatively, each noise may be uniform on an equally spaced grid including both endpoints of $[q,2q]$, and $\eta$ in <a href="#int:probability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="int:probability">[int:probability]</a> may be the largest grid spacing. The same conclusions hold for any set of points reached from $x$ by band paths of length at most $\rho$, including the points and representatives encountered on those paths.
  - Explanation: A function with Lipschitz constant strictly below one on a thin band is approximated by a random one-Lipschitz function made of named point-distance atoms, with a probability bound for the selected atom changing across a small ball.
- **claim-interpolation-accuracy-collars** (theorem, [source](#stmt-int-deterministic)): Every node formula $F_\nu$ has error at most $5\delta$ at every portal of a child of $\nu$, and error at most $6\delta$ on its own layer. At a nonroot node with parent $\pi$, if $$\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_{d_J}(v,K_\nu)\le c_0:=\zeta\delta/50,$$ then $F_\nu(v)=F_\pi(v)$, with the same selected atom name.
  - Explanation: Each node formula has bounded error at child portals and on its own layer, and agrees exactly, with the same atom name, with its parent near its own portals.
- **claim-common-node-formula** (theorem, [source](#stmt-int-common-formula)): For $x\in Y$ and $0<\rho\le\rho_0$, one node $\nu$, determined without the interpolation noises, satisfies $Q=F_\nu$ on $B_{d_J}(x,\rho)$, including equality of selected atom names. If $n$ is the depth of $\nu$, every point of this ball has a representative on a column with $b_i\ge n\delta$.
  - Explanation: On a small enough ball the interpolation coincides, atom names included, with a single node formula that is determined without the noises.
- **claim-bounded-ancestor-depth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-int-depth)): For the node $\nu$ supplied by Lemma <a href="#int:common-formula" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="int:common-formula">19</a>, every atom selected by $F_\nu$ on the ball originates in $\nu$ or one of its last $M$ ancestors. Consequently at most $N$ atom names can be selected there.
  - Explanation: Atoms selected on the ball come only from the node or a bounded number of its last ancestors, so only boundedly many atom names compete.
- **claim-fresh-noise-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-int-near-tie)): For any two distinct named atoms in the deterministic list of Lemma <a href="#int:depth" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="int:depth">20</a>, any fixed $x\in Y$, and $t\ge0$, $$\label{int:near-tie-bound} \Pr\bigl(\|A(x)-A'(x)\|\le t\bigr)\le(2t+\eta)/q.$$ The same bound holds when one atom is replaced by any real quantity independent of all current interpolation noises.
  - Explanation: For two distinct named atoms, or an atom and an independent quantity, the chance of a near tie at a fixed point is linear in the tolerance, thanks to a fresh independent noise.
- **claim-branch-comparison-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-int-comparisons)): Condition on arbitrary prior data. Let $W$ be the union of a nonempty family of band paths issuing from a fixed point $x$, each of length at most $\rho$. The set $W$ and these paths are fixed by the prior data. Suppose that $Q_1,\ldots,Q_s$ are interpolation constructions, each on a band component containing all these paths. Their geometries, inputs, and portals are fixed by the prior data, and their fresh noise families are mutually independent. The bands and their intrinsic metrics may differ; each of the specified paths must be permitted in every band. Denote the constants of $Q_i$ in <a href="#int:constants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="int:constants">[int:constants]</a> by $q_i,N_i,\rho_{0,i}$.* *Allow inputs $c_i+\varepsilon_iQ_i$, with $\varepsilon_i\in\{-1,1\}$ and $c_i$ fixed by the prior data. Also allow one fixed branch $f$ and $t$ branches $g_j+\beta_j$, where $f$ and the $g_j$ are fixed functions whose restrictions to each specified path, parametrized by arclength, are $L$-Lipschitz. The $\beta_j$ are fresh independent uniform noises on intervals of length $w_j>0$, independent also of the interpolation noises. All functions respect the switches used by the paths. Set $$L_* =\max(1,L),\qquad S=1+t+\sum_{i=1}^sN_i,\qquad q_* =\min\bigl(\{q_i:1\le i\le s\}\cup \{w_j:1\le j\le t\}\bigr).$$ Assume that there is at least one randomized input and that $\rho\le\min_i\rho_{0,i}$ when $s>0$. Any fixed expression formed from these inputs by finitely many minima and maxima selects one branch and, for a selected interpolation, one atom throughout $W$, except with conditional probability at most $$\label{int:comparison-probability} \frac{S^2}{q_*}(8L_*\rho+\eta).$$ Continuous noises have $\eta=0$; for equally spaced endpoint grids $\eta$ can be the largest spacing of any noise used here. The expression uses each interpolation with a single chosen sign and shift; repeated appearances of that same input are given the same name.
  - Explanation: Any finite min-max expression of interpolations and fixed or shifted branches selects one branch and one atom throughout a union of short paths, except with a probability bound uniform in prior data.
- **claim-main-replacement-validity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-validity)): The simultaneous replacements <a href="#main:replacement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="main:replacement">[main:replacement]</a> respect switches, preserve vertical nesting and the vertical upper bound for system A, and admit ordinary charts on every new branch. At any point the change in the system-A label set has measure at most $\kappa_A(1+10D)$.
  - Explanation: The simultaneous main replacements respect switches, keep vertical nesting and the upper bound in system A, create ordinary charts on new branches, and move label sets by a bounded measure.
- **claim-main-raise-or-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-ray)): Suppose $w$ satisfies the height and radial interior requirements for its width-$D$ cell, and that the cell is available. For every choice of the interpolation parameters, its kept replacement raises $s(w)$ by more than $2E_A$, unless a descending path at $w$ exists. Every descending path satisfies $$s(\gamma(t))\le s(w)-t+\epsilon \qquad(0\le t\le d_0). \label{main:ray-prefix}$$
  - Explanation: At an interior point of an available cell the kept main replacement raises the field by a definite amount unless an almost maximally descending short path exists there.
- **claim-main-pair-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-pair-separation)): Let $(x,y)$ be an eligible pair satisfying the interior requirements, $1\le d(x,y)\le2$, and $\|s(x)-s(y)\|\le E_A$. If either endpoint has no descending path, then, conditional on main-update mode, the independent cell coins give probability at least $1/4$ of system-A symmetric-difference separation at least $\kappa_A E_A$ at this step.
  - Explanation: If one endpoint of an eligible pair has no descending path, the independent cell coins give a fixed probability of system-A separation of definite size.
- **claim-tip-label-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pack-barrier)): Suppose a retained pair has a non-neighbor link separating its representatives strictly, with tip $e$ at a height in $J_2$, such that $d(e,x)\le1$ or $d(e,y)\le1$. If $$s(e)<s(w)-j \label{pack:drop}$$ for at least one endpoint $w$, then there is label mass at least $\kappa_Aj/4$ for which both endpoints are active and $e$ is inactive. In no-change mode this gives a component-refined separation in system A of at least $\kappa_Aj/2$ at this step.* *More generally, suppose subsequent changes have total unrefined symmetric-difference costs $\Delta_x,\Delta_y,\Delta_e$ at the three points. The final component-refined separation is at least $$2\bigl(\kappa_Aj/4-\Delta_x-\Delta_y-\Delta_e\bigr)_+. \label{pack:barrier-damage}$$
  - Explanation: A separating non-neighbor link whose tip lies sufficiently below an endpoint field value yields a definite label mass on which both endpoints are active and the tip inactive, giving component-refined separation that survives bounded later damage.
- **claim-restricted-packing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pack-bound)): Fix a narrow band $J_2$. In any component of $X_{J_2}$, every closed $d_{J_2}$-ball of radius $4R$ meets seeds from at most $$M_{\mathrm{pack}}=2+\left\lceil1600R/g\right\rceil \label{pack:number}$$ groups. This bound is independent of the graph, the number of columns, and the history of the construction.
  - Explanation: A narrow-band ball of fixed radius meets seeds from only boundedly many groups, a bound independent of the graph, the number of columns and the construction history.
- **claim-ordered-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ord-support)): Suppose that $k,w$ belong to one component of $X_I$. There is an ordered field $L$ of slope bound $1$ such that $$L(k)=0,\qquad L(w)=d_I(k,w),\qquad L(v)\le d_I(k,v)\quad(v\in X_I),$$ where the last inequality uses the convention that distance to a different component is infinite.
  - Explanation: A point-distance function has an ordered one-Lipschitz support below it that vanishes at the source and equals the distance at the target.
- **claim-transverse-field-lipschitz** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ord-transverse-lipschitz)): The field $t_s$ is well defined on $X_I$, respects all switches, and is $4$-Lipschitz for $d_I$.
  - Explanation: The signed transverse field is well defined on the band, respects all switches and has a fixed Lipschitz bound for the intrinsic distance.
- **claim-overlapping-traversals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ord-overlap)): Let $J\subset I$ have width $\eta$. Suppose that $s$ is ordered with slope bound $4$, and that paths $\Gamma_x,\Gamma_y$ in $X_J$ use only columns of the same open arc. Assume that $\Gamma_x$ stays within length $A_x$ of $x$ and $\Gamma_y$ stays within length $A_y$ of $y$. If the range of $s$ on each path contains an interval $[u,v]$ with $$v-u>32\eta,$$ then $$d(x,y)\le A_x+A_y+2\eta.$$
  - Explanation: Two paths on one open arc that both traverse a sufficiently long interval of field values force the two base points to be close in the metric.
- **claim-two-arc-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ord-two-side)): Set $R=10^{-8}$. Let $s$ be an ordered field of slope bound $4$ on $X_I$. Suppose that $x,y\in X_I$ satisfy $$1\le d(x,y)\le2,\qquad d_I(x,y)=d(x,y), \qquad \|s(x)-s(y)\|\le10^{-3}R.$$ Let $J\subset I$ be a common height interval of width $$\eta\le10^{-6}R$$ containing $x,y$. For each $z\in\{x,y\}$, suppose that there are two paths $\gamma_z^-,\gamma_z^+$ in $X_J$, starting at $z$, of length at most $2R$, whose terminal points $z^-,z^+$ satisfy $$s(z^-)<s(z)-0.4R,\qquad s(z^+)>s(z)+0.4R.$$ Then $t_s(x),t_s(y)$ have opposite signs and $$0.01R\le \|t_s(x)\|,\|t_s(y)\|\le8.$$
  - Explanation: If both endpoints of a far pair have short paths on which the ordered field falls and rises, they lie on opposite arcs, so their transverse values have opposite signs and bounded magnitude.
- **claim-secondary-clipping-validity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sec-validity)): The secondary update preserves the nested-set and chart invariants. Its movement at each point, measured in symmetric difference, is bounded by a universal constant in scale units.
  - Explanation: Secondary clipping preserves nested sets and chart invariants and moves label sets by a universal amount in scale units.
- **claim-admitted-seed-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sec-admitted-success)): If a surviving pair has a seed admitted for one comparison orientation, then the no-change and secondary-clipping modes together give a probability bounded below by a positive universal constant of separating that pair by a positive universal amount.
  - Explanation: When a surviving pair has an admitted seed, the no-change and clipping modes together separate it by a definite amount with probability bounded below by a universal constant.
- **claim-increment-lipschitz-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-sec-increment-bound)): If $Q>2000/R$, all increments of one ordered identifier together have $d_I$-Lipschitz constant at most $K=100$. They preserve the nested sets, introduce no chart interfaces, and move each point by at most a universal constant times the scale.
  - Explanation: With a large enough scale ratio all increments of one ordered identifier have a fixed total Lipschitz bound, keep nesting, add no chart interfaces and move points by a bounded multiple of the scale.
- **claim-remaining-pairs-increment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sec-remaining)): Suppose a surviving pair has no admitted endpoint in either ordered orientation. Then the increment mode separates it by a positive universal amount with uniformly positive probability.
  - Explanation: If no endpoint is admitted in either orientation, the ordered field rises and falls near both endpoints, and the increment mode separates the pair by a definite amount with uniformly positive probability.
- **claim-movement-and-success** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pad-success)): There are constants $M<\infty$ and $b,p>0$ such that the following hold at every step of scale $r$.* 1. *At every point of $X$, in either system, the symmetric-difference movement of the unrefined set is at most $Mr$.* 2. *Given any past for which a vertex pair at distance in $[r,2r)$ is eligible, the step has probability at least $p$ of producing one of these certificates:* 1. *unrefined symmetric-difference separation at least $br$ in one system;* 2. *a non-neighbor tip $e$ and a set of labels of measure at least $br$ in system $A$ on which both pair vertices are active and $e$ is inactive, with the vertices strictly on opposite sides of that link.
  - Explanation: Per scale, label movement is bounded by a multiple of the scale, and every eligible pair at that scale obtains a separation certificate with probability bounded below.
- **claim-chart-persistence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pad-persistence)): There exist constants $C_0<\infty$ and $\rho_0>0$ such that the following holds. Condition on the past at a step of scale $r$. Suppose a fixed closed ball $B_d(v,\rho r)$ lies in a single old region in each system. For $0<\rho\le\rho_0$, the probability that this ball is split between different new regions in either system is at most $$\label{pad:loss} C_0(\rho+\eta_0).$$ Here $\eta_0$ is the relative mesh allowance from Section <a href="#sets:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sets:setup">5.2</a>.
  - Explanation: A small ball lying in one old chart region in each system is split between new regions with probability linear in its relative radius plus the mesh allowance.
- **claim-almost-embeddable-families** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-almost-embeddable)): Fix nonnegative integers $g,k,w,a$, and interpret $\mathrm{AE}(g,(k,w),a)$ exactly as the almost-embeddable family of Lee and Sidiropoulos (Lee and Sidiropoulos 2009, Theorem 1.7) [[LS09](#ref-LS09)]. Let $h_0=h_0(g,k,w,a)\in\mathbb N_0$ be the target treewidth bound and let $D=D(g,k,w,a)\ge1$ be a finite stochastic distortion bound supplied there. Put $$\widehat h=\max\{h_0,1\},\qquad K(g,k,w,a)=D\max\{C_{\mathrm{pl}},C_{\widehat h+1}\},$$ where $C_{\mathrm{pl}}$ is the constant of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, and $C_t$ is the companion’s constant for bags of cardinality at most $t\ge2$. Every finite connected simple graph $G$ in this named family, with strictly positive real edge lengths, admits a map $f$ into a real $L_1$ space satisfying $$d_G(u,v)\le\\|f(u)-f(v)\\|_1 \le K(g,k,w,a)d_G(u,v)\qquad(u,v\in V(G)).$$ For the same underlying graph, $K(g,k,w,a)$ also bounds the undirected fractional concurrent flow–cut gap $\phi_*/\lambda_*$ for the finite nonnegative capacities and demands defined in Section <a href="#intro:flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:flow">1.1</a>, whenever $\lambda_*>0$.
  - Explanation: For each fixed almost-embeddable family, combining the planar theorem, the bounded-treewidth companion and the stochastic reduction gives an L1 embedding with a family-dependent distortion bound, which also bounds the fractional concurrent flow–cut gap when the optimal multiplier is positive.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sets-component-refinement",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a system of sets is nested increasingly on each column, respects switches, and has symmetric-difference measure at most vertical length. It defines an $L_1$ pseudometric dominated by $d$. It also admits the following refinement with the same upper bound.*\n\n*For a label $\\omega$, join two columns when one of their switches occurs at a height active for $\\omega$. To an active point assign the unit vector indexed by its component in this finite graph, and to an inactive point assign zero. Integrating the $\\ell_1$ distances of these vectors gives the refined pseudometric. It dominates the original symmetric-difference pseudometric.*\n\n*If a non-neighbor link has inactive tip and two active points have representatives strictly on opposite sides of it, their refined distance for that label is $2$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-int-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0<\\zeta<1$, let $E>0$, and set $$\\label{int:constants}\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\delta=E/8,\\qquad a_0=10^{-6}\\zeta\\delta,\\qquad\n q=\\zeta\\delta/20,\\\\\n \\rho_0=\\zeta\\delta/1000,\\qquad\n M=\\lceil15/\\zeta\\rceil+3,\\qquad N=6(M+1).\n \\end{gathered}$$ Suppose $0<a\\le a_0$, and let $p:Y\\to\\mathbb R$ be $(1-\\zeta)$-Lipschitz for $d_J$. There is a random function $Q:Y\\to\\mathbb R$, obtained using finitely many independent scalar noises uniform on $[q,2q]$, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For every realization, $Q$ respects all switches, is $1$-Lipschitz for $d_J$, and satisfies $|Q(v)-p(v)|\\le6\\delta<E$.*\n\n2.  *Its pieces use finitely many named atoms of the form $$\\label{int:atom-form}\n     A(v)=C+\\varepsilon d_J(k,v),\\qquad \\varepsilon\\in\\{-1,1\\},\\quad k\\in Y.$$ Each name specifies a source $k$ and sign $\\varepsilon$ fixed before the noises are sampled; the intercept $C$ is random. Whenever the construction reuses an atom, it retains its name.*\n\n\n3.  *For a fixed $x\\in Y$ and $0<\\rho\\le\\rho_0$, there is a list $\\mathcal A(x,\\rho)$ of at most $N$ atom names and sources, determined without the noises, that contains the name of every atom selected by $Q$ on $B_{d_J}(x,\\rho)$. Except on an event of probability at most $$\\label{int:probability}\n     \\frac{N^2}{q}(8\\rho+\\eta),$$ one named atom is selected throughout this ball.*\n\n*Here $\\eta=0$ for continuous uniform noises. Alternatively, each noise may be uniform on an equally spaced grid including both endpoints of $[q,2q]$, and $\\eta$ in <a href=\"#int:probability\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"int:probability\">[int:probability]</a> may be the largest grid spacing. The same conclusions hold for any set of points reached from $x$ by band paths of length at most $\\rho$, including the points and representatives encountered on those paths.",
        "source_id": "PM-089.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-int-comparisons",
        "exact_quote": "Condition on arbitrary prior data. Let $W$ be the union of a nonempty family of band paths issuing from a fixed point $x$, each of length at most $\\rho$. The set $W$ and these paths are fixed by the prior data. Suppose that $Q_1,\\ldots,Q_s$ are interpolation constructions, each on a band component containing all these paths. Their geometries, inputs, and portals are fixed by the prior data, and their fresh noise families are mutually independent. The bands and their intrinsic metrics may differ; each of the specified paths must be permitted in every band. Denote the constants of $Q_i$ in <a href=\"#int:constants\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"int:constants\">[int:constants]</a> by $q_i,N_i,\\rho_{0,i}$.*\n\n\n*Allow inputs $c_i+\\varepsilon_iQ_i$, with $\\varepsilon_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}$ and $c_i$ fixed by the prior data. Also allow one fixed branch $f$ and $t$ branches $g_j+\\beta_j$, where $f$ and the $g_j$ are fixed functions whose restrictions to each specified path, parametrized by arclength, are $L$-Lipschitz. The $\\beta_j$ are fresh independent uniform noises on intervals of length $w_j>0$, independent also of the interpolation noises. All functions respect the switches used by the paths. Set $$L_* =\\max(1,L),\\qquad\n S=1+t+\\sum_{i=1}^sN_i,\\qquad\n q_* =\\min\\bigl(\\{q_i:1\\le i\\le s\\}\\cup\n                         \\{w_j:1\\le j\\le t\\}\\bigr).$$ Assume that there is at least one randomized input and that $\\rho\\le\\min_i\\rho_{0,i}$ when $s>0$. Any fixed expression formed from these inputs by finitely many minima and maxima selects one branch and, for a selected interpolation, one atom throughout $W$, except with conditional probability at most $$\\label{int:comparison-probability}\n \\frac{S^2}{q_*}(8L_*\\rho+\\eta).$$ Continuous noises have $\\eta=0$; for equally spaced endpoint grids $\\eta$ can be the largest spacing of any noise used here. The expression uses each interpolation with a single chosen sign and shift; repeated appearances of that same input are given the same name.",
        "source_id": "PM-089.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-main-ray",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $w$ satisfies the height and radial interior requirements for its width-$D$ cell, and that the cell is available. Every descending path satisfies $$s(\\gamma(t))\\le s(w)-t+\\epsilon\n \\qquad(0\\le t\\le d_0).\n \\label{main:ray-prefix}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-089.1"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Distortion at most C** ([source](#intro)): An embedding of a finite metric space $(V,d)$ into real $L_1$ has *distortion at most $C$* if, after multiplication by a positive scalar, its distances lie between $d$ and $Cd$.
- **Flow–cut gap** ([source](#intro:flow)): For $\lambda_*>0$, the ratio $\phi_*/\lambda_*$ is the *flow–cut gap*.
- **Column model** ([source](#stmt-geo-column-model)): A *column model* consists of a finite cyclically ordered set $V_0$ of indices, a copy $\{i\}\times\mathbb R$ of the height line for each $i\in V_0$, and a finite collection of links.
- **Noncrossing links** ([source](#stmt-geo-column-model)): The links are *noncrossing* if no two have four distinct endpoints alternating in the cyclic order.
- **Terminal switch** ([source](#stmt-geo-column-model)): We additionally require that every link between nonconsecutive indices is a switch at a single height $H_e$, and that neither endpoint column has any switch above $H_e$.
- **Active column graph** ([source](#stmt-geo-column-model)): The active column graph $G_I$ has vertex set $V_0$ and the links whose switch sets meet $I$ as edges.
- **Radial cell** ([source](#stmt-geo-cell-definition)): A *cell* is the image in $X_J$ of all intervals $\{i\}\times J$ belonging to one such component.
- **Chart** ([source](#pm-h-15)): A *chart* consists of a region $\mathcal R\subset X$, a height band $I$ containing its heights, a field $P$ on the corresponding component of $X_I$ (or on all of $X_I$), and measurable functions $Z:\Omega\to\mathbb R$ and $\sigma:\Omega\to\{-1,1\}$ whose joint pushforward measure is Lebesgue measure times a fair sign.
- **Ordinary chart** ([source](#pm-h-15)): $$ This describes exactly the same sets by a chart with field zero. ◻ </details> In an *ordinary chart*, the field has the form $P=c+\eta s$, where $\eta\in\{-1,1\}$ and either $s(v)=d_I(o,v)$ for a fixed source $o$, or $s=0$.
- **Eligible pair** ([source](#pm-h-17)): At scale $r$, a pair $(x,y)$ is *eligible* if the two closed balls $B_d(x,5r)$ and $B_d(y,5r)$ lie in one common old chart region in system $A$ and in one common old chart region in system $B$.
- **Descending path** ([source](#stmt-main-ray-definition)): A descending path at a point $w$ is a shortest path in $J_1$, of length $d_0\in[10^{-5},0.02]$, whose terminal point $z$ satisfies $$s(z)\le s(w)-d_0+\epsilon. \label{main:ray-endpoint}$$ It is parametrized by arclength $t\in[0,d_0]$. All descending paths used below lie in the old ordinary chart band, so $s$ can be evaluated at every point of the path.
- **Ordered field** ([source](#stmt-ord-definition)): Let $a,b$ be two distinct column indices. A real field $s$ on $X_I$ is *ordered from $a$ to $b$*, with slope bound $M$, if it respects every switch, is $M$-Lipschitz on every vertical column, and, at each height $H\in I$, is nondecreasing on each of the two circular arcs oriented from $a$ to $b$. In particular, the value on column $a$ is a global minimum at that height, and the value on column $b$ is a global maximum. We call the two open arcs the positive and negative arcs, with either assignment of these names.
- **Transverse field** ([source](#stmt-ord-transverse-definition)): Let $s$ be ordered from $a$ to $b$ with slope bound $4$. In $\mathbb R^2$ define the set $$\begin{split} Z_s={}&\{(s(a,H),H),(s(b,H),H):H\in I\}\\ &{}\cup\{(s(p,H),H): H\in I\cap S_{pq},\ p\text{ and }q\text{ lie on opposite open arcs}\}. \end{split}$$ For a representative $(i,H)$ define $$t_s(i,H)= \begin{cases} \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty((s(i,H),H),Z_s),&i\text{ on the positive arc},\\ -\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty((s(i,H),H),Z_s),&i\text{ on the negative arc},\\ 0,&i\in\{a,b\}. \end{cases}$$ Here $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty$ is distance for the maximum norm on $\mathbb R^2$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-universal-constant | Universal distortion constant C | ≥ | $1$ | independent of vertex count and edge lengths |
| q-final-constant | Distortion constant from the final assembly | = | $16m/(pb)$ | depends only on the fixed construction constants |
| q-portal-cover-radius | Distance from an entrance column point to its nearest portal | ≤ | $200a$ | any threshold t with entrance columns |
| q-cell-weak-diameter | Weak diameter of a radial cell | ≤ | $10D$ | cell width D |
| q-interpolation-error | Uniform error of the interpolation | ≤ | $6\delta$ | every realization of the noises |
| q-atom-list-size | Number of candidate atom names on a small ball | ≤ | $N=6(M+1)$ | zeta in (0,1) |
| q-main-label-movement | Change of the system-A label set at a point under the main replacement | ≤ | $\kappa_A(1+10D)$ | scale units |
| q-packing-groups | Number of seed groups meeting a narrow-band ball of radius 4R | ≤ | $2+\left\lceil1600R/g\right\rceil$ | independent of the graph and construction history |
| q-increment-lipschitz | Lipschitz constant of all increments of one ordered identifier | ≤ | $100$ | scale ratio Q large |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-geodesics** [claim](#stmt-geo-geodesics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-geodesics): Shorten paths that revisit a column, then minimize over finitely many column words with continuous switch heights. -> metric and attained geodesics
- **step-realization** [claim](#stmt-geo-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-realization): Shortest-path tree heights, doubled non-tree edges with identified tips, columns in contour order. -> noncrossing terminal-switch column model
- **step-chord** [claim](#stmt-geo-chord-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-chord-separation): An arc-changing edge would alternate with the chord, contradicting noncrossing. -> chord separation
- **step-gap** [claim](#stmt-geo-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-gap): Apply chord separation to an edge joining distinct gaps. -> paths stay in one gap
- **step-skipping** [claim](#stmt-geo-skipping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-skipping): Take the first edge crossing the index and apply chord separation. -> visit or non-neighbor link
- **step-outerplanar** [claim](#stmt-geo-outerplanar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-outerplanar): Cone over the chord drawing is planar; a K_{2,3} minor would give K_{3,3} by an edge count. -> outerplanarity
- **step-sublevel** [claim](#stmt-geo-sublevel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-sublevel): Truncate shortest paths and move vertically to the common height. -> connected sublevel sets
- **step-portals** [claim](#stmt-geo-portals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-portals): Three separated entrances give disjoint connectors and a K_{2,3} minor. -> two portals
- **step-cells** [claim](#stmt-geo-cells) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-cells): Finite unions of compact intervals; geodesic to the root exits through an entrance near a portal. -> cell compactness and diameter
- **step-padding** [claim](#stmt-geo-padding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-padding): Triangle inequality on radial values keeps short paths in one layer. -> padding and frontier distance
- **step-portal-levels** [claim](#stmt-geo-portal-levels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-portal-levels): Nested superlevel components and the root-geodesic argument at each threshold. -> portal tree distances
- **step-vertical** [claim](#stmt-sets-vertical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sets-vertical): Half-line symmetric differences averaged over signs; rebasing by inverse maps. -> distance identity and rebasing
- **step-component-refinement** [claim](#stmt-sets-component-refinement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sets-component-refinement): Component coordinates cost no more than membership; tip inactivity forces different components by noncrossing. -> refined pseudometric
- **step-deterministic** [claim](#stmt-int-deterministic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-int-deterministic): Collar calculation plus induction on tree depth with envelope bounds. -> accuracy and equality collars
- **step-common-formula** [claim](#stmt-int-common-formula) / [proof](#proof-stmt-int-common-formula): Connected projected ball lies in one superlevel node; equality collar handles the second depth. -> single node formula
- **step-depth** [claim](#stmt-int-depth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-int-depth): A distant node cannot pass its parent atom through while staying accurate. -> bounded ancestor horizon
- **step-near-tie** [claim](#stmt-int-near-tie) / [proof](#proof-stmt-int-near-tie): Condition on all noises but the later atom's own noise. -> anti-concentration bound
- **step-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-int-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-int-near-tie-2): Union bound over atom pairs; constant selection on a connected ball. -> stability probability
- **step-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-int-comparisons) / [proof](#proof-stmt-int-comparisons): Pairwise comparisons with independence across constructions, then constancy along paths. -> min-max stability
- **step-main-validity** [claim](#stmt-main-validity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-main-validity): Strict margins at frontier and height ends; Lipschitz products below one. -> valid main replacement
- **step-ray** [claim](#stmt-main-ray) / [proof](#proof-stmt-main-ray): A small raise forces a nearby minimizing frontier point whose shortest path descends. -> raise or descending path
- **step-pair-separation** [claim](#stmt-main-pair-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-main-pair-separation): Keep one endpoint's cell coin and suppress the other. -> probability of separation
- **step-barrier** [claim](#stmt-pack-barrier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pack-barrier): Threshold comparison on one sign half yields label mass; component refinement doubles it. -> component-refined separation
- **step-packing** [claim](#stmt-pack-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pack-bound): Low connected set, circular gap, two extreme paths, skipped seeds near the skipping link, padding count. -> group count bound
- **step-ord-support** [claim](#stmt-ord-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ord-support): Running minima toward the target on each arc, using connected strict sublevels. -> ordered support
- **step-transverse-lipschitz** [claim](#stmt-ord-transverse-lipschitz) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ord-transverse-lipschitz): Distance to a fixed set is one-Lipschitz; check switches case by case. -> well-defined Lipschitz field
- **step-overlap** [claim](#stmt-ord-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ord-overlap): Monotone reference profile plus skipping and chord separation. -> endpoints close in metric
- **step-two-side** [claim](#stmt-ord-two-side) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ord-two-side): Empty rectangle for the switch-image set, strip argument, overlap contradiction. -> opposite signs and bounds
- **step-sec-validity** [claim](#stmt-sec-validity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sec-validity): Margins at distance boundaries and height ends; clipping argument. -> valid secondary update
- **step-admitted** [claim](#stmt-sec-admitted-success) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sec-admitted-success): Isolate the designated group using the packing bound; compare modes. -> separation probability
- **step-increment** [claim](#stmt-sec-increment-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sec-increment-bound): Disjoint supports at distinct scales under a large scale ratio. -> Lipschitz bound
- **step-remaining** [claim](#stmt-sec-remaining) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sec-remaining): Failed admissions give rise and fall paths; two-arc lemma gives opposite signs. -> increment separation
- **step-pad-success** [claim](#stmt-pad-success) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pad-success): Case split over cutoffs, descending paths, barrier exclusion, admission. -> movement and success
- **step-persistence** [claim](#stmt-pad-persistence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pad-persistence): Height bands, main clipping and secondary clipping, using the comparison corollary. -> chart persistence
- **step-final-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-44): Geometric schedule sums, mixture over schedules, finite cut representation and rescaling. -> planar L1 embedding
- **step-almost-embeddable** [claim](#stmt-cor-almost-embeddable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-almost-embeddable): Positive-length perturbation limit, one-sum composition and stochastic reduction. -> family embedding bound

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-branch-lengths**: For every non-tree edge of a weighted connected graph with root heights h given by shortest-path distances from the root, the two attached branches have nonnegative lengths (l+h(v)-h(u))/2 and (l+h(u)-h(v))/2, and these lengths sum to the edge length l.
- **inv-tip-height**: The two branch tips of a non-tree edge have the common height (l+h(u)+h(v))/2, and every branch length is nonnegative.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-nonplanar-columns**: Apply the column-model bookkeeping to a graph that is not planar and expect noncrossing links. -> The bookkeeping of branch lengths still runs, but the noncrossing circular order of the links is only guaranteed by a plane embedding.
- **anti-embedding-claim**: Read the verified branch-length computation as an algorithm producing the final L1 embedding with the universal constant. -> The final embedding is built from a randomized multi-scale construction; the harness covers only the first deterministic reduction.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The column realization of a planar graph is an explicit finite procedure: a shortest-path tree, root heights, and closed-form branch lengths and tip heights for each non-tree edge. This arithmetic part is executable and checkable on small graphs; the randomized embedding is not covered.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin: 'n m' then m lines 'u v l' (vertices 0..n-1, positive integer lengths, connected, simple); root is vertex 0.
# PRECONDITIONS: graph connected, no parallel edges, lengths positive integers.
# OUTPUTS: line 1: root distances h(0..n-1); then for every non-tree edge in input order: 'index 2a 2b 2H' (doubled branch lengths and tip height).
# TIME: O(n*m) Bellman-Ford style relaxation plus O(n*m) tree selection.
# SPACE: O(n+m).
# ARITHMETIC: exact integer arithmetic only; halves are avoided by doubling.
# FAILURE: disconnected input leaves infinite heights and is outside the contract.
import sys
def main():
    data=sys.stdin.read().split()
    n=int(data[0]); m=int(data[1])
    E=[]
    for i in range(m):
        E.append((int(data[2+3*i]),int(data[3+3*i]),int(data[4+3*i])))
    INF=float('inf')
    h=[INF]*n
    h[0]=0
    for _ in range(n):
        for (u,v,l) in E:
            if h[u]+l<h[v]: h[v]=h[u]+l
            if h[v]+l<h[u]: h[u]=h[v]+l
    tree=set()
    for v in range(1,n):
        for i,(a,b,l) in enumerate(E):
            if b==v and h[a]+l==h[v]:
                tree.add(i); break
            if a==v and h[b]+l==h[v]:
                tree.add(i); break
    out=[" ".join(str(x) for x in h)]
    for i,(u,v,l) in enumerate(E):
        if i not in tree:
            out.append("%d %d %d %d"%(i,l+h[v]-h[u],l+h[u]-h[v],l+h[u]+h[v]))
    print("\n".join(out))
main()
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys\ndef main():\n    try:\n        inp=open(sys.argv[1]).read().split()\n        cand=open(sys.argv[2]).read()\n        exp=sys.argv[3]\n        if cand.strip()!=exp.strip():\n            sys.exit(1)\n        n=int(inp[0]); m=int(inp[1])\n        E=[(int(inp[2+3*i]),int(inp[3+3*i]),int(inp[4+3*i])) for i in range(m)]\n        BIG=10**9\n        d=[[BIG]*n for _ in range(n)]\n        for i in range(n): d[i][i]=0\n        for u,v,l in E:\n            d[u][v]=min(d[u][v],l); d[v][u]=min(d[v][u],l)\n        for k in range(n):\n            for i in range(n):\n                for j in range(n):\n                    if d[i][k]+d[k][j]<d[i][j]: d[i][j]=d[i][k]+d[k][j]\n        lines=cand.strip().split(\"\\n\")\n        h=[int(x) for x in lines[0].split()]\n        if h!=d[0]:\n            sys.exit(1)\n        listed=set()\n        for ln in lines[1:]:\n            t=[int(x) for x in ln.split()]\n            if len(t)!=4: sys.exit(1)\n            i,a2,b2,H2=t\n            if i<0 or i>=m or i in listed: sys.exit(1)\n            listed.add(i)\n            u,v,l=E[i]\n            if a2!=l+h[v]-h[u] or b2!=l+h[u]-h[v] or H2!=l+h[u]+h[v]: sys.exit(1)\n            if a2<0 or b2<0 or a2+b2!=2*l: sys.exit(1)\n        tree=[i for i in range(m) if i not in listed]\n        if len(tree)!=n-1: sys.exit(1)\n        par=list(range(n))\n        def find(x):\n            while par[x]!=x:\n                par[x]=par[par[x]]; x=par[x]\n            return x\n        for i in tree:\n            u,v,l=E[i]\n            if abs(h[u]-h[v])!=l: sys.exit(1)\n            ru,rv=find(u),find(v)\n            if ru==rv: sys.exit(1)\n            par[ru]=rv\n        sys.exit(0)\n    except SystemExit:\n        raise\n    except Exception:\n        sys.exit(1)\nmain()\n",
    "output_contract": "Root distances on the first line, then one line per non-tree edge with index and doubled branch lengths and doubled tip height.",
    "reason": "The column realization of a planar graph is an explicit finite procedure: a shortest-path tree, root heights, and closed-form branch lengths and tip heights for each non-tree edge. This arithmetic part is executable and checkable on small graphs; the randomized embedding is not covered.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: stdin: 'n m' then m lines 'u v l' (vertices 0..n-1, positive integer lengths, connected, simple); root is vertex 0.\n# PRECONDITIONS: graph connected, no parallel edges, lengths positive integers.\n# OUTPUTS: line 1: root distances h(0..n-1); then for every non-tree edge in input order: 'index 2a 2b 2H' (doubled branch lengths and tip height).\n# TIME: O(n*m) Bellman-Ford style relaxation plus O(n*m) tree selection.\n# SPACE: O(n+m).\n# ARITHMETIC: exact integer arithmetic only; halves are avoided by doubling.\n# FAILURE: disconnected input leaves infinite heights and is outside the contract.\nimport sys\ndef main():\n    data=sys.stdin.read().split()\n    n=int(data[0]); m=int(data[1])\n    E=[]\n    for i in range(m):\n        E.append((int(data[2+3*i]),int(data[3+3*i]),int(data[4+3*i])))\n    INF=float('inf')\n    h=[INF]*n\n    h[0]=0\n    for _ in range(n):\n        for (u,v,l) in E:\n            if h[u]+l<h[v]: h[v]=h[u]+l\n            if h[v]+l<h[u]: h[u]=h[v]+l\n    tree=set()\n    for v in range(1,n):\n        for i,(a,b,l) in enumerate(E):\n            if b==v and h[a]+l==h[v]:\n                tree.add(i); break\n            if a==v and h[b]+l==h[v]:\n                tree.add(i); break\n    out=[\" \".join(str(x) for x in h)]\n    for i,(u,v,l) in enumerate(E):\n        if i not in tree:\n            out.append(\"%d %d %d %d\"%(i,l+h[v]-h[u],l+h[u]-h[v],l+h[u]+h[v]))\n    print(\"\\n\".join(out))\nmain()\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "0 2 3\n2 4 2 8",
        "input": "3 3\n0 1 2\n0 2 3\n1 2 3\n"
      },
      {
        "expected": "0 1 2",
        "input": "3 2\n0 1 1\n1 2 1\n"
      },
      {
        "expected": "0",
        "input": "1 0\n"
      },
      {
        "expected": "0 1 2 1\n2 0 2 4",
        "input": "4 4\n0 1 1\n1 2 1\n2 3 1\n0 3 1\n"
      },
      {
        "expected": "0 3 2 5\n0 8 2 8\n3 7 1 11",
        "input": "4 5\n0 1 5\n1 2 1\n0 2 2\n2 3 4\n1 3 2\n"
      }
    ]
  },
  "pm_id": "PM-089.1",
  "schema": "pm.triptych.v2"
}

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves that an embedding exists and does not give a library-ready procedure. Its random cut construction relies on chosen constants and on continuous label spaces, and no interface or reference implementation is given. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the source concerns simulation, game worlds or real-time geometry. The planar graphs are abstract weighted metrics, not scenes, and the output is an abstract measure-space embedding. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The column model and the chart bookkeeping are proof devices. No checker, visualiser or analysis tool is specified, and nothing the paper builds would work as a standalone utility. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main result is existential: a universal constant exists, and no value for it is computed. The construction runs through random local updates over many scales, with no stated running time. No finite procedure with a checkable contract is given. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The charts, column models and tree of superlevel components are analytical bookkeeping. They are not given as updatable data structures with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardware, parallelism or memory-access aspect appears in the source. The proof works with measurable label sets and probability estimates, not with data layouts or kernels. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The randomness used is an analytical device for expected separation, not a source of secrecy or hardness. No adversary, hardness assumption or security notion appears. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The embedding is built for graph metrics and has no learning component. It does not describe training, generalisation or learned representations, and the target space is real L1 with a fixed constant distortion bound. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The distortion bound would transfer to a bound on the planar flow-cut gap, so the result bears on optimisation theory. It still supplies no routing or cut-finding procedure, and the constant is not explicit, so there is no construction to implement. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper treats static finite metrics and has no communication model, failure model or protocol. Nothing in the source concerns message passing or consensus. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home of the result is metric geometry and graph theory, for example proving the planar case of a conjecture about minor-closed families. It is not an engineering contract in a further domain. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-planar-positive-lengths** (hypothesis): The universal distortion bound is stated only for finite connected planar graphs with strictly positive real edge lengths. Non-planar graphs, disconnected graphs, zero-length edges and infinite graphs are outside the theorem. Blocked use: ['Applying the constant to non-planar network topologies', 'Applying the constant to graphs with zero-length edges or infinitely many vertices']
- **limit-constant-not-explicit** (unknown): The final constant is expressed through construction constants (the number of schedules, a success probability and a gain constant). The paper evaluates no numerical value, so no concrete numeric distortion guarantee can be quoted. Blocked use: ['Quoting a numeric distortion value for a specific graph', 'Using the constant for sizing or tolerance decisions in practice']
- **limit-randomized-nonconstructive-assembly** (nonconstructive): The embedding is a probability mixture over schedules and over a finite tree of random outcomes. It proves existence of a finite cut representation, and no procedure with a stated running time is given for producing it. Blocked use: ['Presenting the construction as a polynomial-time embedding algorithm', 'Claiming a deterministic or derandomized procedure']
- **limit-thin-band-width** (hypothesis): The cell diameter, padding and portal estimates require the band width to be small relative to the cell width. Outside that regime the radial cells carry no diameter guarantee. Blocked use: ['Using the cell diameter bound on thick height bands', 'Reusing the portal and cell estimates when the band width is comparable to the cell width']
- **limit-narrow-band-packing** (formal_scope): The packing bound on seed groups is proved inside a single narrow band and only for seeds of surviving witness pairs. It does not give bounded overlap of all broad-band cells. Blocked use: ['Reading the bound as a packing estimate for the whole metric', 'Applying it to seeds in different bands or to non-surviving pairs']
- **limit-increment-scale-ratio** (hypothesis): The Lipschitz bound for the combined increments needs a sufficiently large spacing between successive scales. With close scales the supports of different increments can overlap and the bound is lost. Blocked use: ['Running schedules with closely spaced scales and assuming the increment bound', 'Using the bound without checking the stated scale ratio condition']
- **limit-no-clique-sum-closure** (formal_scope): The paper does not establish closure under general clique sums, so it does not prove the full conjecture for all minor-closed families. Only the planar case and the fixed-parameter almost-embeddable corollary are covered. Blocked use: ['Claiming constant L1 distortion for all graphs excluding a fixed minor', 'Treating this paper as a proof of the full GNRS conjecture']
- **limit-euclidean-target** (edge_case): The result concerns embeddings into L1 only. Lower bounds are cited showing that planar or even series-parallel metrics can need growing distortion in Euclidean space. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem to claim constant-distortion Euclidean embeddings', 'Feeding planar metrics to Euclidean-embedding pipelines expecting dimension-free guarantees']
- **limit-almost-embeddable-fixed-parameters** (hypothesis): The family corollary holds for fixed nonnegative integer parameters, and the resulting constant depends on them. If genus, apex count or fringe parameters grow with the graph size, no uniform bound follows. Blocked use: ['Applying the corollary to families with parameters that grow with the vertex count', 'Treating the family constant as independent of the parameters']
- **limit-flow-cut-positive-multiplier** (edge_case): The flow–cut consequence is a statement about the undirected fractional concurrent version and is only meaningful when the largest routable multiplier is positive. It says nothing about integral routing or directed networks. Blocked use: ['Using it for integral or single-path routing', 'Using it for directed graphs or for instances with zero routable multiplier']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 089 (`lean/docs/089.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Planar Graph Metrics Embed into $L_1$ with Constant Distortion](../../preprints/Planar-Graph-Metrics-Embed-into-L1-with-Constant-Distortion-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [$L_1$ Embeddings of Graphs of Bounded Treewidth](../../preprints/L1-Embeddings-of-Graphs-of-Bounded-Treewidth-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The planar case of the Gupta–Newman–Rabinovich–Sinclair embedding problem asks for a universal distortion bound in $L_1$. The formalized result gives one constant $C$ for every finite connected planar graph with arbitrary positive real edge lengths: its weighted shortest-path metric embeds into real $L_1$ with distortion at most $C$. The constant is independent of the number of vertices and the ratios between edge lengths; singleton graphs are included.
>
> The formalized result gives a uniform $L_1$ embedding bound for each bounded-treewidth class. For every bag-size bound $k\ge2$, there is a constant $C(k)$ such that the weighted shortest-path metric of every nonempty finite connected graph with a tree decomposition of bag size at most $k$ embeds into finite-dimensional real $L_1$ with distortion at most $C(k)$. Edge lengths may be arbitrary positive real numbers, and the constant is independent of the graph and decomposition size.

| `IsPlanar` | def |
| `walkLength` | def |
| `graphDistance` | def |
| `MainStatement` | def |
| `planar_graph_metrics_embed_L1` | theorem |

Comparator source `PlanarL1.lean` (2176 bytes, sha256 `e902947b3b86e48e2eb3052f5832ee4a3ac5fb4e4ffd3ad0b8ad16f66344c3c3`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace PlanarL1

/-- A topological drawing by simple plane arcs, with injective vertex placement,
no vertex in an edge interior, and disjoint interiors for distinct unoriented edges. -/
def IsPlanar {V : Type} (G : SimpleGraph V) : Prop :=
  ∃ (point : V → ℝ × ℝ) (arc : ∀ u v : V, G.Adj u v → C(unitInterval, ℝ × ℝ)),
    Function.Injective point ∧
    (∀ u v h, (arc u v h) 0 = point u ∧ (arc u v h) 1 = point v) ∧
    (∀ u v h, Function.Injective (arc u v h)) ∧
    (∀ u v h (t : unitInterval) w, 0 < (t : ℝ) → (t : ℝ) < 1 →
      (arc u v h) t ≠ point w) ∧
    (∀ u v h x y k (s t : unitInterval),
      0 < (s : ℝ) → (s : ℝ) < 1 → 0 < (t : ℝ) → (t : ℝ) < 1 →
      (arc u v h) s = (arc x y k) t →
      (u = x ∧ v = y) ∨ (u = y ∧ v = x))

/-- The sum of edge lengths along a finite graph walk. -/
def walkLength {V : Type} {G : SimpleGraph V} (length : V → V → ℝ)
    {u v : V} : G.Walk u v → ℝ
  | .nil => 0
  | @SimpleGraph.Walk.cons _ _ u w _ _ p => length u w + walkLength length p

/-- The infimum of lengths of actual walks between the two vertices. -/
noncomputable def graphDistance {V : Type} (G : SimpleGraph V)
    (length : V → V → ℝ) (u v : V) : ℝ :=
  sInf (Set.range (fun p : G.Walk u v => walkLength length p))

/-- One universal distortion bound for finite connected planar graphs with
arbitrary symmetric positive edge lengths, realized in a real `L¹` space. -/
def MainStatement : Prop :=
  ∃ C : ℝ, 1 ≤ C ∧
    ∀ (n : ℕ) (G : SimpleGraph (Fin n)) (length : Fin n → Fin n → ℝ),
      G.Connected → IsPlanar G →
      (∀ u v, length u v = length v u) →
      (∀ u v, G.Adj u v → 0 < length u v) →
      ∃ (Ω : Type) (measurable : MeasurableSpace Ω)
          (μ : @MeasureTheory.Measure Ω measurable)
          (f : Fin n → @MeasureTheory.Lp Ω ℝ measurable _ 1 μ),
        ∀ x y, graphDistance G length x y ≤ ‖f x - f y‖ ∧
          ‖f x - f y‖ ≤ C * graphDistance G length x y

theorem planar_graph_metrics_embed_L1 : MainStatement := by
  sorry

end PlanarL1

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every finite connected planar graph with arbitrary positive real edge lengths embeds into real <i>L</i><sub>1</sub> with a universal distortion bound. This resolves the planar embedding conjecture positively.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
